Just tired of spinning my wheels.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain tasks or situations because of feeling overwhelmed by your responsibilities?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I avoid them. I can easily spend twenty minutes just going down some unrelated design rabbit hole before I realize I've completely stopped working on the code I was supposed to be writing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself so intensely focused on a task that you lose track of time and forget about everything else?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't think I ever get that kind of intense focus. \n\nLike I said, my problem is usually the opposite. I can't stay on one thing for very long before something else catches my attention. I never really get so absorbed in a task that I lose track of time or forget about everything else around me. I'm usually too easily distracted for that to happen.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or worthless about your inability to focus or complete tasks?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel worthless or guilty. It's frustrating, sure. I mean, I know I should be getting certain things done, and it's annoying when I realize I've wasted an hour on something completely unrelated. But I don't beat myself up over it or feel like a failure. It's just... a hassle I deal with. I know I'm capable of doing the work, I just struggle to stay on a straight path to finish it.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had difficulty staying focused, or is this a relatively new challenge for you?\n\nPatient: It's definitely always been there. Looking back, I had the exact same issues in grade school. I remember my teachers would always write on my report cards that I was bright but spent too much time daydreaming. I was constantly forgetting to turn in assignments that I had actually finished, or I'd just completely lose my homework folders. So, yeah, this was happening long before I was even a teenager. It's just always been how my brain works, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Especially at work. When I'm on long design calls, I start to feel this... pressure building up in my body to just get up and move. I usually end up pacing around my office, or I'll keep shifting from chair to chair, even when I’m trying my best to look focused on the screen. It's just really hard to sit completely still for that long.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It's really just annoying that I know what needs to be done, but I can't seem to get everything lined up and actually focus on the right things. So, no, not really anxious. Just tired of spinning my wheels.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain tasks or situations because of feeling overwhelmed by your responsibilities?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I avoid them. Like I mentioned, it's not really a feeling of being overwhelmed that stops me. I don't look at a big task and run away from it. \n\nIt's more that... I just get sidetracked. I'll sit at my desk fully intending to work on the workshop prep, but then I see an email from a team member and I just pivot to that instead. So I'm still working, and I'm not hiding from my responsibilities. I'm just not doing the ones I actually sat down to do.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find your mind wandering when you're trying to focus on a task?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. It happens all the time. \n\nI'll be right in the middle of writing a piece of code, and if Slack pings, or a browser tab flashes... or honestly, even if just a visual idea for one of my side projects pops into my head, I completely lose the thread of what I was doing. I can easily spend twenty minutes just going down some unrelated design rabbit hole before I realize I've completely stopped working on the code I was supposed to be writing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself so intensely focused on a task that you lose track of time and forget about everything else?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't think I ever get that kind of intense focus. \n\nLike I said, my problem is usually the opposite. I can't stay on one thing for very long before something else catches my attention. I never really get so absorbed in a task that I lose track of time or forget about everything else around me. I'm usually too easily distracted for that to happen.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or worthless about your inability to focus or complete tasks?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel worthless or guilty. And then we had this difficult architecture review, and because I wasn't fully prepared or focused, it really didn't go well. I've managed to hold onto my position so far.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any problems in your personal relationships, such as with your wife or friends, that you think are directly because of your difficulty staying focused or following through on things?\n\nPatient: I think we just touched on that, actually. It's really just annoying that I know what needs to be done, but I can't seem to get everything lined up and actually focus on the right things. So, no, not really anxious. Just tired of spinning my wheels.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain tasks or situations because of feeling overwhelmed by your responsibilities?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I avoid them. Like I mentioned, it's not really a feeling of being overwhelmed that stops me. I don't look at a big task and run away from it. \n\nIt's more that... I just get sidetracked. I'll sit at my desk fully intending to work on the workshop prep, but then I see an email from a team member and I just pivot to that instead. So I'm still working, and I'm not hiding from my responsibilities. I'm just not doing the ones I actually sat down to do.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find your mind wandering when you're trying to focus on a task?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. It happens all the time. \n\nI'll be right in the middle of writing a piece of code, and if Slack pings, or a browser tab flashes... or honestly, even if just a visual idea for one of my side projects pops into my head, I completely lose the thread of what I was doing. I can easily spend twenty minutes just going down some unrelated design rabbit hole before I realize I've completely stopped working on the code I was supposed to be writing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself so intensely focused on a task that you lose track of time and forget about everything else?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't think I ever get that kind of intense focus. \n\nLike I said, my problem is usually the opposite. I can't stay on one thing for very long before something else catches my attention. I never really get so absorbed in a task that I lose track of time or forget about everything else around me. I'm usually too easily distracted for that to happen.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or worthless about your inability to focus or complete tasks?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel worthless or guilty. It's frustrating, sure. I mean, I know I should be getting certain things done, and it's annoying when I realize I've wasted an hour on something completely unrelated. But I don't beat myself up over it or feel like a failure. It's just... a hassle I deal with. I know I'm capable of doing the work, I just struggle to stay on a straight path to finish it.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had difficulty staying focused, or is this a relatively new challenge for you?\n\nPatient: It's definitely always been there. Looking back, I had the exact same issues in grade school. I remember my teachers would always write on my report cards that I was bright but spent too much time daydreaming. I was constantly forgetting to turn in assignments that I had actually finished, or I'd just completely lose my homework folders. So, yeah, this was happening long before I was even a teenager. It's just always been how my brain works, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Especially at work. When I'm on long design calls, I start to feel this... pressure building up in my body to just get up and move. I usually end up pacing around my office, or I'll keep shifting from chair to chair, even when I’m trying my best to look focused on the screen. It's just really hard to sit completely still for that long.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any problems in your relationships with friends, family, or coworkers due to your difficulty staying focused or getting things done?\n\nPatient: Yeah, mostly at work with my coworkers. It's actually started to damage my professional reputation. I, uh... I recently missed a pretty important patent filing checkpoint because I just lost track of the steps. And then we had this difficult architecture review, and because I wasn't fully prepared or focused, it really didn't go well. It actually left my collaborators questioning whether I can handle leading the larger project we're supposed to be doing. So, yeah, it's definitely causing some real problems for me professionally.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any difficulties in your personal relationships with friends or family due to your struggles with focus or follow-through?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, my wife sometimes gets annoyed if I forget to take out the trash or if I zone out when she's talking about her day, but... it's nothing major. We've been married a long time, so I think she's just used to it by now. My friends don't seem to mind either. It's really just work where it's causing actual problems.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been fired or demoted from a job due to your difficulties with focus or follow-through?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't been fired or demoted. I mean, like I said, it's definitely causing some tension and people are questioning my reliability right now, but it hasn't come to anything official like losing my job or getting knocked down a level. I've managed to hold onto my position so far.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any problems in your personal relationships, such as with your wife or friends, that you think are directly because of your difficulty staying focused or following through on things?\n\nPatient: I think we just touched on that, actually. But, uh, no, not really. Like I mentioned a minute ago, my wife gets a little annoyed sometimes if I forget to do a chore around the house or if I tune out during a conversation, but it hasn't caused any real problems between us. And my friends haven't said anything or seemed bothered. So, no, nothing major on the personal side.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever lost a friend due to your inability to follow through on plans or maintain focus in social interactions?\n\nPatient: No, never. I've never lost a friend over anything like that. Like I said, my friends really don't seem bothered by it. It's just work where this is an issue.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had to significantly adjust your work or personal life to accommodate your difficulties with focus and follow-through?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really done anything like that. I mean, I haven't made any major changes or rearranged my life around it. I just try to push through my work day as best I can and deal with the delays as they happen. So, no, no big adjustments to how I live or work.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Looking back, I had the exact same issues in grade school. I remember my teachers would always write on my report cards that I was bright but spent too much time daydreaming. I was constantly forgetting to turn in assignments that I had actually finished, or I'd just completely lose my homework folders. So, yeah, this was happening long before I was even a teenager. It's just always been how my brain works, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Especially at work. When I'm on long design calls, I start to feel this... pressure building up in my body to just get up and move. I usually end up pacing around my office, or I'll keep shifting from chair to chair, even when I’m trying my best to look focused on the screen. It's just really hard to sit completely still for that long.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any problems in your relationships with friends, family, or coworkers due to your difficulty staying focused or getting things done?\n\nPatient: Yeah, mostly at work with my coworkers. It's actually started to damage my professional reputation. I, uh... I recently missed a pretty important patent filing checkpoint because I just lost track of the steps. And then we had this difficult architecture review, and because I wasn't fully prepared or focused, it really didn't go well. It actually left my collaborators questioning whether I can handle leading the larger project we're supposed to be doing. So, yeah, it's definitely causing some real problems for me professionally.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any difficulties in your personal relationships with friends or family due to your struggles with focus or follow-through?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, my wife sometimes gets annoyed if I forget to take out the trash or if I zone out when she's talking about her day, but... it's nothing major. We've been married a long time, so I think she's just used to it by now. My friends don't seem to mind either. It's really just work where it's causing actual problems.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been fired or demoted from a job due to your difficulties with focus or follow-through?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't been fired or demoted. I mean, like I said, it's definitely causing some tension and people are questioning my reliability right now, but it hasn't come to anything official like losing my job or getting knocked down a level. I've managed to hold onto my position so far.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any problems in your personal relationships, such as with your wife or friends, that you think are directly because of your difficulty staying focused or following through on things?\n\nPatient: I think we just touched on that, actually. But, uh, no, not really. Like I mentioned a minute ago, my wife gets a little annoyed sometimes if I forget to do a chore around the house or if I tune out during a conversation, but it hasn't caused any real problems between us. And my friends haven't said anything or seemed bothered. So, no, nothing major on the personal side.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever lost a friend due to your inability to follow through on plans or maintain focus in social interactions?\n\nPatient: No, never. I've never lost a friend over anything like that. Like I said, my friends really don't seem bothered by it. It's just work where this is an issue.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had to significantly adjust your work or personal life to accommodate your difficulties with focus and follow-through?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really done anything like that. I mean, I haven't made any major changes or rearranged my life around it. I just try to push through my work day as best I can and deal with the delays as they happen. So, no, no big adjustments to how I live or work.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had to turn down a social invitation or a work opportunity because of your difficulties with focus or follow-through?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I've never turned down a project at work because of this. I just... I take the work, and then I end up struggling to get it done on time. And socially, no, I still go out when I'm invited. So it hasn't really stopped me from accepting things, it just makes actually doing them a bit harder once I'm committed.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I don't think I ever get that kind of intense focus. \n\nLike I said, my problem is usually the opposite. I can't stay on one thing for very long before something else catches my attention. Just tired of spinning my wheels.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain tasks or situations because of feeling overwhelmed by your responsibilities?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I avoid them. Like I mentioned, it's not really a feeling of being overwhelmed that stops me. I don't look at a big task and run away from it. \n\nIt's more that... I just get sidetracked. I'll sit at my desk fully intending to work on the workshop prep, but then I see an email from a team member and I just pivot to that instead. So I'm still working, and I'm not hiding from my responsibilities. I'm just not doing the ones I actually sat down to do.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find your mind wandering when you're trying to focus on a task?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. It happens all the time. \n\nI'll be right in the middle of writing a piece of code, and if Slack pings, or a browser tab flashes... or honestly, even if just a visual idea for one of my side projects pops into my head, I completely lose the thread of what I was doing. I can easily spend twenty minutes just going down some unrelated design rabbit hole before I realize I've completely stopped working on the code I was supposed to be writing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself so intensely focused on a task that you lose track of time and forget about everything else?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't think I ever get that kind of intense focus. \n\nLike I said, my problem is usually the opposite. I can't stay on one thing for very long before something else catches my attention. I never really get so absorbed in a task that I lose track of time or forget about everything else around me. I'm usually too easily distracted for that to happen.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or worthless about your inability to focus or complete tasks?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel worthless or guilty. It's frustrating, sure. I mean, I know I should be getting certain things done, and it's annoying when I realize I've wasted an hour on something completely unrelated. But I don't beat myself up over it or feel like a failure. It's just... a hassle I deal with. I know I'm capable of doing the work, I just struggle to stay on a straight path to finish it.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had difficulty staying focused, or is this a relatively new challenge for you?\n\nPatient: It's definitely always been there. Just tired of spinning my wheels.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain tasks or situations because of feeling overwhelmed by your responsibilities?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I avoid them. Like I mentioned, it's not really a feeling of being overwhelmed that stops me. I don't look at a big task and run away from it. \n\nIt's more that... I just get sidetracked. I'll sit at my desk fully intending to work on the workshop prep, but then I see an email from a team member and I just pivot to that instead. So I'm still working, and I'm not hiding from my responsibilities. I'm just not doing the ones I actually sat down to do.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find your mind wandering when you're trying to focus on a task?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. It happens all the time. \n\nI'll be right in the middle of writing a piece of code, and if Slack pings, or a browser tab flashes... or honestly, even if just a visual idea for one of my side projects pops into my head, I completely lose the thread of what I was doing. I don't think I ever get that kind of intense focus. \n\nLike I said, my problem is usually the opposite. I can't stay on one thing for very long before something else catches my attention. I never really get so absorbed in a task that I lose track of time or forget about everything else around me. I'm usually too easily distracted for that to happen.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or worthless about your inability to focus or complete tasks?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel worthless or guilty. It's frustrating, sure. I mean, I know I should be getting certain things done, and it's annoying when I realize I've wasted an hour on something completely unrelated. I know I'm capable of doing the work, I just struggle to stay on a straight path to finish it.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had difficulty staying focused, or is this a relatively new challenge for you?\n\nPatient: It's definitely always been there. I'm usually too easily distracted for that to happen.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or worthless about your inability to focus or complete tasks?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel worthless or guilty. I was constantly forgetting to turn in assignments that I had actually finished, or I'd just completely lose my homework folders. So, yeah, this was happening long before I was even a teenager. I usually end up pacing around my office, or I'll keep shifting from chair to chair, even when I’m trying my best to look focused on the screen. It's just really hard to sit completely still for that long.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any problems in your relationships with friends, family, or coworkers due to your difficulty staying focused or getting things done?\n\nPatient: Yeah, mostly at work with my coworkers. And then we had this difficult architecture review, and because I wasn't fully prepared or focused, it really didn't go well. It actually left my collaborators questioning whether I can handle leading the larger project we're supposed to be doing.
